- What changed in `.kiro/specs/llm-probe/design.md`:
  - DA-1: an in-browser size rule with three threshold options. S: ≤ 1,024 MiB, ≤ 1.2 B, ≤ 2,048 MiB VRAM. M (recommended): ≤ 1,536 MiB, ≤ 2.3 B, ≤ 2,600 MiB. L: ≤ 2,560 MiB, ≤ 3.1 B, ≤ 3,072 MiB.
  - Under M the candidates are WebLLM and transformers.js Qwen3.5-0.8B and Qwen3.5-2B, plus transformers.js LFM2.5-1.2B (pending the Author's acceptance of its custom license). gemma-4-E2B, Ministral-3-3B and SmolLM3-3B are dropped as too heavy, and granite-4.0-1b is dropped under M for its 1,699 MiB q4 download.
  - Workers AI under Free: gemma-4-26b-a4b-it, glm-4.7-flash and granite-4.0-h-micro are kept. qwen3.8-27b (typical locked run 37,863 neurons), gpt-oss-20b, llama-4-scout and the Paid-only models are dropped.

### Spec: llm-probe — design revised after Author review 1 (2026-11-06)
- `.kiro/specs/llm-probe/design.md` brought in line with the revised `requirements.md`. Every review item (B1 to B6, M1 to M12, the minors) is mapped to the design sections it changed in a table under the new note "Revised 2026-11-06 (Author review 1)".
- Main changes:
  - Ledger once-only rule keyed on the Model_Key (runtime, model ID, quantization, Pinned_Revision): any overlapping entry, complete or incomplete, allows only `--resume` or `--rerun-reason` (with `rerun_of` and `overlaps`); the Report uses the earliest complete entry per Model_Key × Task_Type.
  - New "Locked run flow": Manifest_Check, gates, once-only check, Dev_Run precondition, pre-render of every Card × Permutation, Fresh_Profile and quota check, ledger start, cold start on Warm_Up_Prompts, card requests; `complete` only with no remaining `not_run`.
  - Renderer: `Today is day {{tick}}.`, `, due day N` on events with a `due_tick`, `none` for empty optional fields, render errors structural only; `RENDERER_VERSION` 2.
  - Workers AI adapter: per-model `response_shape` and output-token parameter, re-read on the developers.cloudflare.com model pages and output schemas: gemma-4-26b-a4b-it and glm-4.7-flash use the chat shape and `max_completion_tokens`; granite-4.0-h-micro uses the `response` text shape and `max_tokens`. Raw text only from the content field; `empty_content`, `truncated`, `non_text_response`; no `JSON.stringify`; usage mapping including `reasoning_tokens`; smoke check of the declared shape gates later runs.
  - Browser driver with `chromium.launchPersistentContext(mkdtemp dir, …)`, warm cache via `page.reload()`, quota check through `navigator.storage.estimate()`, profile deleted in `finally`; performance figures taken at Locked_Run start.
  - D constraint is one card-independent schema `{"option": string}`. WebLLM issue 560 was re-read: it is closed, and a maintainer reported it fixed in npm 0.2.66; not verified on the Target_Machine.
  - Timing state machine for Generation_Cap, Late_Answer, Idle_Cap and `runtime_stuck`; outcome mapping for `budget`, `not_approved`, `gpu_unavailable`; pinned weights (commit sha, commit-pinned model library URL, `resolve/<sha>/`); thinking disabled with recorded `chat_template_kwargs`; VRAM_Preflight prompt length and `min_new_tokens` 1,024; ONNX Runtime placement logging with CPU nodes flagged; Exclusion_Records as a terminal Phase_1 state; new `check-web-imports` gate with a `?raw` fault injection planned; Intel memory named "Chrome GPU process peak RSS (excludes unmapped GEM buffers)" in its own column.
  - Minors: integer nearest-rank percentile index, three weak-evidence labels with the 10-point margin, the stale second-review note marked superseded, `excluded: [{id, reason}]` in the grades file, the `llm-probe-` schema prefix on every record, approvals read only from git `HEAD` with a clean file, one redaction sink for every writer, the conversion-repo license note, DA-8 gemma S-only typical corrected to 3,969 (with a rounding note), and the DA-9 note that the rotation direction was chosen after the lock and before any model run.
  - Properties 3, 14, 19, 20, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 29, 32, 35, 36, 37 and 38 revised; Property 39 (Workers AI response extraction) added; prework re-run for the changed criteria.
- A section "Contradictions fixed in this revision" lists 17 contradictions with the revised requirements that were found and fixed, plus one tension (R8.2 versus R11.11 for Workers AI) resolved by taking the more specific criterion.
- Awaiting Author confirmation (implemented as written, not yet confirmed):
  1. R8.13: at the Idle_Cap no Late_Answer is recorded and later requests on that engine return `runtime_stuck` until the engine is rebuilt.
  2. M10 / R9.1: CPU-placed ONNX Runtime nodes are flagged, not failed.
  3. M6 / R9.16: the VRAM_Preflight prompt length is bounded by the longest rendered Dev_Card prompt (at least 8,000 bytes), because rendering Locked_Card prompts outside a Locked_Run would break R3.1.

### Spec: llm-probe — requirements: Author confirmations (2026-11-06)
- The Author answered the three "awaiting confirmation" items from the design revision above. `.kiro/specs/llm-probe/requirements.md` was edited in place; numbering kept.
  1. Idle_Cap and `runtime_stuck`: confirmed. The timed-out request stays a Timeout (Illegal). Before the next request the Probe_Runner rebuilds the engine and runs the Warm_Up_Prompts. If the rebuild fails, the whole Combination is recorded as one failure, and its remaining runs are not counted card by card as Illegal in any Metric. A `runtime_stuck` record is not expected in normal operation; if one occurs it is an ungraded engine fault listed in the Report. Changed: R8.13 (extended), R8.5 (exception for `runtime_stuck` and the remaining runs after a failed rebuild), R15.7 (engine rebuild failures and `runtime_stuck` faults listed).
  2. CPU-placed ONNX Runtime nodes: flagged, not failed, confirmed, because the game meets the same fallback. The Report must say that a flagged Combination's latency figures are not pure-WebGPU results and, if it is recommended, list this as a risk. Changed: R9.1 (sentence appended), new R15.11 and R15.12.
  3. VRAM_Preflight prompt length: margin added. The prompt is at least max(8,000 bytes, 2 × the longest rendered Dev_Card prompt), and its synthetic context holds at least 8 events and 6 persons. The Author supplied aggregate maxima only (context JSON D 2,146 dev / 3,092 locked bytes, S 2,019 / 2,394 bytes; S events 6 / 8; persons 5 / 6), so no Locked_Card was rendered outside a Locked_Run and R3.1 holds. The VRAM_Preflight result records prompt length, event count and person count. Changed: R9.16 and the VRAM_Preflight glossary entry.
- The Author also listed unverified items to be checked before the formal runs (WebLLM issue 560 at 0.2.87, `enable_thinking` in the Qwen3.5 template, gemma-4 / glm-4.7 reasoning fields, granite truncation by `completion_tokens`, DA-8 neuron estimates for glm and granite, ONNX Runtime placement logging, actual WebLLM download size, re-reading WebLLM at the v0.2.87 tag). These go into `tasks.md`, which is not started; no requirement changed for them.
- `validate_spec_format` reports no issues. `design.md` and `tasks.md` not edited; nothing committed.

### Spec: llm-probe — design: Author confirmations and pre-run verification list (2026-11-06)
- `.kiro/specs/llm-probe/design.md` brought in line with the requirements update above (R8.5, R8.13, R9.1, R9.16, R15.7, R15.11, R15.12, glossary VRAM_Preflight). The "Awaiting Author confirmation" list is now marked decided (Author, 2026-11-06), and a new note "Revised 2026-11-06 (Author review 2)" maps each change to the sections it touched.
- Idle_Cap: the timing state machine gains `Rebuilding` and `Failed` states. After an Idle_Cap hit the runner rebuilds the engine and runs both Warm_Up_Prompts before the next request. A failed rebuild writes one Combination failure record `rebuild_failed`, an `abort` ledger event and `not_run` (`rebuild_failed`) for the remaining runs, none graded. A `runtime_stuck` refusal becomes a new outcome kind `engine_fault`: not graded, not Illegal, in no Metric, listed in the Report. Outcome mapping, phase gate, Metrics, Report section 10, Results record and Error Handling updated.
- CPU-placed nodes: flagged, not failed. Report section 6 adds a "not pure-WebGPU results" note to the latency rows, and section 9 adds a "Risks" line when a flagged Combination is recommended.
- VRAM_Preflight: prompt at least max(8,000 bytes, 2 × longest rendered Dev_Card prompt), with a synthetic context of at least 8 events and 6 persons. The Author-supplied aggregates (dev / locked: D context 2,146 / 3,092 bytes, S context 2,019 / 2,394 bytes, S events 6 / 8, persons 5 / 6) are recorded as the basis. The preflight record gains `event_count` and `person_count`.
- Property 40 (engine faults never reach a Metric) added; Property 29 unchanged. Testing Strategy gains example tests for rebuild success, rebuild failure (one failure record, no per-card Illegal grade), forced `runtime_stuck`, the new preflight fields and the two new Report lines.
- New section "Pre-run verification items" (V1 to V8) lists the Author's eight unverified items with when to verify and the fallback, to become tasks that must be done before the Phase_1 Locked_Runs.
- `validate_spec_format` reports no issues. `tasks.md` not started; nothing committed.

- `.kiro/specs/llm-probe/tasks.md` written from the approved `requirements.md` and `design.md`, in the style of the web-shell plan: 26 parent tasks, 151 leaf sub-tasks, one wave per leaf in the Task Dependency Graph.
- Parent tasks:
  1. `sim_core::llm` types, strict JSON reader, Card parser (Property 1).
  2. Output_Validator (Properties 2 to 6, forbidden-literal scan).

  7. Manifest_Check and card loading (Properties 16, 17; fault injection: one hex digit of the pinned constant).
  8. Results, ledger, budget and approvals storage with redaction (Properties 24, 27, 28, 32).
  9. Metrics and statistics (Properties 18 to 23; Unit_Tests fault injection: Wilson `z` = 1.0).
  10. Phase gate and Report generator (Property 37).
  11. Deploy leak checks and the `web/`-imports-`eval/` gate (Property 35; four fault injections).
  12. Adapter_Interface, timing state machine, fallback chain (Properties 29, 31).
  13. Run flow, ingest, re-grade (Properties 25, 26, 30, 33, 34, 38, 40).
  14. Checkpoint.
  15. Pre-run verification V1 to V8.
  16. In-browser adapters, Probe_Page, browser driver, preflight and screen (Property 36).
  17. Workers AI adapter and the `smoke` command (Property 39).
  18. to 26. Measurement and closing: budget approval, screening and VRAM_Preflight, Dev_Runs and template freeze, Phase_1 Locked_Runs, Phase_1 checkpoint, Phase_2, Report, runtime decision and steering, final audit and push.
- Stop-and-ask points:
  - 18.1: Request_Budget and Neuron_Cap approval, after V5.
  - 19.1: before `screen` and the VRAM_Preflights.
  - 20.1 and 20.6: before the Dev_Runs, and before freezing the templates.
  - 21.2 to 21.6: one approval per Phase_1 Locked_Run, each with kiro-log start and end entries.
  - 22.1: Phase_1 checkpoint and the Phase_2 branch decision.
  - 23.2 and 23.3: branch B `dev` and `locked` approvals.
  - 24.2: Report review.
  - 25.1: runtime decision.
  - 26.6: deploy approval before pushing `main`.
  - Headed Chrome only if headless WebGPU fails (20.1).
- Where V1 to V8 sit (task 15, sub-tasks 15.1 to 15.8 = V1 to V8, scheduled by the graph):
  - V8 runs after task 14 and before the WebLLM adapter (task 16).
  - V3 and V4 are Smoke_Calls after the Workers AI adapter (task 17), within the 10-per-day count.
  - V5 runs after them and before the budget approval (18.1).
  - V6 runs at the first VRAM_Preflight (19.3).
  - V1 and V7 run at the first WebLLM Dev_Run (20.2, with V7 also at 20.4); V2 runs at the first transformers.js Dev_Run (20.3).
  - All are required before 21.1.

### Spec: llm-probe — task 3.8 fault injection: Grader ignores must_not_include (2026-11-06)

- Gate under test: `cargo test --workspace` in `scripts/gates.sh`, specifically the Property 8 test (`s_grades_agree_with_the_reference_model`) and the Grader examples.
- Injected fault: in `crates/sim-core/src/llm/grade.rs`, `satisfies` stopped checking the `exclude` (`must_not_include`) patterns.
- First attempt: my one-line edit was not rustfmt-clean, so the run stopped earlier at `gate failed: cargo fmt --check` (exit 1). I reformatted the injection and ran again.
- Result of the second run: `cargo test --workspace` failed in the `llm_examples` binary (3 example tests: `synthesis_acceptable_needs_every_include_and_no_exclude`, `synthesis_contrast_pair_uses_include_and_exclude_patterns`, `relationship_shift_pattern_matches_only_with_a_and_b_swapped`), then `gate failed: cargo test --workspace`, exit code 101. Cargo stops after the first failing test binary, so Property 8 did not run inside the gate.
- Property 8 check: running `cargo test -p sim-core --test llm_properties` by hand with the same fault failed `s_grades_agree_with_the_reference_model` (and `contrast_decisions_follow_the_expected_subsets`) with a minimal failing input listing a non-empty `must_not_include`. It wrote `llm_properties.proptest-regressions`, which I deleted.
- Revert: restored `grade.rs` from a backup copy; `grep` finds no injection and `satisfies` again has the `!exclude.iter().any(...)` line.
- Re-run: `scripts/gates.sh` printed `All gates passed.`, exit 0.

### Spec: llm-probe — task 5.3 grading fixtures generator location (2026-11-06)

- Choice: the generator is two `sim-cli` subcommands in `crates/sim-cli/src/grading.rs`: `grading-outputs` (writes `fixtures/grading/dev-outputs.json`) and `grading-golden` (writes `fixtures/grading/dev-golden.json`). `scripts/regen-grading-golden.sh` runs both. Reason: `sim-cli` is native tooling that already owns `scripts/regen-fixtures.sh`, and it can call `sim_core::llm::api` directly, so the golden bytes come from the same code the exports wrap.
- Both files are deterministic (sorted keys, stable order, trailing newline). A `sim-cli` black-box test regenerates them into a temp directory and compares with the committed files; it never writes the fixtures. Only `dev.jsonl` is read.

### Spec: llm-probe — task 13 design and assumption decisions (2026-11-07)

Four decisions made while implementing task 13 that later tasks must honour:

- (a) The locked `ingest` command completes the ledger entry through `appendComplete`. The page-bundle flow only writes `start`; ingest writes the matching `complete`.
- (b) Ingest does not write an `llm-probe-perf` record. The Report must read cold-start figures from `eval/results/ingest/<run-id>.json`, or task 16 must make the driver/ingest also write the perf records. Task 16 decides.
- (c) `cards.ts`: an unknown void ID is an error only when both splits are loaded. With one split loaded, a void ID that is not in it is not treated as an error.
- (d) The `RuntimeAdapter` interface has `identity`, `state` and `complete` only. Lifecycle (create, rebuild, warm-up) sits on the engine and in `adapters/lifecycle.ts`.

### Spec: llm-probe — V8: WebLLM interfaces re-read at v0.2.87 (2026-11-07)

Task 15.8. The design note ("Re-read at the `v0.2.87` tag" in Research findings, plus the revised WebLLM bullet in "In-browser adapters") was written by a first attempt that timed out before logging and committing. A second attempt finished here: it spot-checked the note against the tag with `gh api` and found no wrong claim, so the note was left unchanged.

Verified at the tag (no change needed):
- `cleanModelUrl` (`src/support.ts:92-98`) keeps a URL that matches `/resolve/<ref>/`, so a pinned-commit URL is fetched at that commit.
- `extra_body.enable_thinking` and `ignore_eos` exist in `chat_completion.ts`.
- `vram_required_MB` is 1894.19 (`Qwen3.5-0.8B-q4f32_1-MLC`) and 2591.55 (`Qwen3.5-2B-q4f32_1-MLC`), as written.

Differs from the earlier design wording (now fixed in the design):
1. `response_format.schema` is a JSON-encoded string, accepted only with `type: "json_object"`.
2. Prebuilt Qwen3.5 entries carry `overrides` (`context_window_size: 4096`, `max_history_size: 1`, `conv_config.stop_token_ids: [248046, 248044]`) that a custom entry must repeat.
3. `vram_required_MB` is metadata only; the VRAM_Preflight is the only size check.
4. An interrupt applies only to the running request and ends with `finish_reason: "abort"`.
5. `ModelRecord` has new optional `integrity` and `model_manifest` fields, unused here.

Only items 1, 2 and the numbers above were re-checked in this attempt. The line references for items 3 to 5 come from the first attempt and were not re-checked.

### Spec: llm-probe — V3: Workers AI smoke on gemma-4 and glm-4.7 (2026-11-07)

Partial: only gemma-4 was run (3 of the 6 planned Smoke_Calls); glm-4.7-flash was NOT run because the first run returned an HTTP 400 on the synthesis call and the instruction was to stop at the first error response. Smoke_Calls used today: 3 of 10.

Added `smoke --diagnose` (off by default; commit f84b598). It records key names only (`response_keys`: top, result, choice, message) and the numeric fields of `result.usage` (`usage_detail`), no values or text. Real calls on `workers-ai/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` (`chat_template_kwargs` `enable_thinking: false` sent, `max_completion_tokens` 64 for D, 1024 for S):
- D-dream_interpretation-001: HTTP 200, shape_check ok (`chat`), finish_reason `stop`, usage 826 in / 8 out, `reasoning_tokens` absent, verdict `decision`, 1050 ms. `usage_detail`: completion_tokens 8, prompt_tokens 826, total_tokens 834, `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` 0, `neurons` 7.727. No `completion_tokens_details` key.
- S-grudge_detection-001: HTTP 400, outcome `remote_error`, verdict illegal (`runtime_error`), no usage. Response top-level keys `errors`, `messages`, `result`, `success`. The error code and message are not recorded, so the cause (for example the synthesis `json_schema` constraint or the 1024 `max_completion_tokens`) is not known yet.
- D-dream_interpretation-002: HTTP 200, shape_check ok, finish_reason `stop`, usage 827 in / 8 out, `reasoning_tokens` absent, verdict `decision`, 757 ms. `usage_detail` the same keys, `neurons` 7.736.
- Message key names: call 1 `content, role`; call 3 `annotations, audio, content, function_call, reasoning, refusal, role` (a `reasoning` key exists in the message on this call). Choice keys: call 1 `finish_reason, index, logprobs, message`; call 3 adds `routed_experts, stop_reason, token_ids`. Result keys: call 1 `choices, created, id, model, object, usage`; call 3 adds `ec_transfer_params, kv_transfer_params, metrics, prompt_logprobs, prompt_text, prompt_token_ids, service_tier`. The key set differs between two 200 responses of the same model.
- Neurons: the estimate was 31 per D call and 65 for the S call. The API-reported `neurons` was about 7.73 per D call. The budget counter shows `requests` 3, `smoke` 3, `neurons` 81 (the failed S call has no usage, so its 65 estimate stays reserved).
- `combinations.json` and `response_shape` were not changed.

### Spec: llm-probe — V3 (continued): cause of the gemma-4 synthesis 400, glm-4.7 shape check (2026-11-07)

Smoke_Calls used today: 7 of 10 (3 left for V4: granite normal check + truncation probe, one spare). Code commit 58b645e.

Tooling. `smoke --diagnose` now also records, for a non-2xx response, `api_error` (first `errors[0].code` and the Cloudflare `message` text only, through `scrub`, cut to 300 chars) and `request_facts` (`response_format_sent`, `response_format_type`, `max_tokens_param`, `max_tokens_value`, `chat_template_kwargs_sent`, `prompt_bytes`; read in memory from the body that was sent, names and numbers only). New option `--task D|S` sends only that Task_Type (not with `--max-tokens`). Unit tests with a fake fetch cover both.

Cause of the S 400 (gemma-4, one diagnosed call): API code 8007, message `Grammar error: Unimplemented keys: ["uniqueItems"]` (BadRequestError). Request facts: `response_format` sent with type `json_schema`, `max_completion_tokens` 1024, `chat_template_kwargs` sent, prompt 3788 bytes. So the cause was in our request body: the locked synthesis schemas contain `uniqueItems: true` on `evidence`, and the Workers AI grammar compiler does not implement that keyword. The D calls never hit it (the decision schema has no `uniqueItems`). The other keywords in the four synthesis schemas (`const`, `enum`, `minItems`, `pattern`, `minimum`, `additionalProperties`, `required`, `$schema`, `$id`, `title`, `description`) were not reported, and the message lists the unimplemented keys, so they appear accepted; the successful retry below agrees.
Fix (adapter only, `eval/probe/src/adapters/workers-ai.ts`): `buildRequestBody` sends a deep copy of the schema without `uniqueItems` (`UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA_KEYS`, property names untouched, input not mutated). The prompt text still shows the original schema and the Output_Validator still enforces uniqueness on the answer, so grading is unchanged. `eval/cards/` untouched. Retest: one gemma S call, HTTP 200, shape ok, finish_reason `stop`, 1142 in / 160 out, verdict `synthesis` (Output_Validator accepted), 3987 ms. This applies to every Workers AI Combination with `constraint: json_schema` (granite too). Effect on the experiment: json_schema constraint on Workers AI is a looser grammar than the locked schema for this one keyword; record it in the report.

glm-4.7-flash, `--count 2 --diagnose` (D then S; the planned third call, a second D, was skipped to keep 3 calls in reserve for V4). `chat_template_kwargs` `enable_thinking: false` sent, `max_completion_tokens` 64 / 1024:
- D-dream_interpretation-001: HTTP 200, shape ok, finish_reason `stop`, 768 in / 7 out, verdict `decision`, 1607 ms. API `neurons` 4.479 (estimate 21).
- S-grudge_detection-001: HTTP 200, shape ok, finish_reason `stop`, 1046 in / 85 out, verdict `synthesis`, 1483 ms. API `neurons` 8.847 (estimate 60).
- Key names (both calls identical): message `annotations, audio, content, function_call, reasoning, reasoning_content, refusal, role, tool_calls`; choice `finish_reason, index, logprobs, message, stop_reason, token_ids`; result `choices, created, id, kv_transfer_params, model, object, prompt_logprobs, prompt_token_ids, service_tier, system_fingerprint, usage`. `usage_detail` has only `completion_tokens`, `prompt_tokens`, `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (0), `total_tokens`, `neurons`; no `completion_tokens_details`, no reasoning count.

V3 answers.
(a) Reasoning field key names: glm-4.7 returns both `message.reasoning` and `message.reasoning_content` on every call (keys only; values were not read, so whether they were empty is not known). gemma-4 returns `message.reasoning` on some responses (one of its 3 successful calls) and not on others; the same model gave different key sets on calls a few seconds apart. The adapter reads only `message.content`, so these fields never reach the outcome.
(b) Reasoning tokens billed: no usage record on any call (6 successful calls) has a reasoning-token field, and `completion_tokens` equals a short answer length (gemma D 8, glm D 7). If thinking had run and been billed as output, D calls would show far more than 7-8 completion tokens. Not proven: there was no control call with `enable_thinking` true or absent, so "both models read `enable_thinking: false`" is an inference from low output counts, not a measured fact.
(c) Shape check: 6 of 6 successful calls `shape_check` ok on `chat`; finish_reason `stop` on all 6; no `length` seen (no truncation probe was run on these two).
(d) Neurons per call vs estimate: gemma D 7.73 and 7.74 (estimate 31), gemma S 14.75 (estimate 65), glm D 4.48 (estimate 21), glm S 8.85 (estimate 60). The estimates (built from prompt bytes and the max output cap) are 4-7x the reported cost. The budget counter reads `neurons` 175 for 7 requests: it includes 65 each for the two failed gemma S calls (no usage, estimate left reserved), so it over-counts real use by about 130.
(e) Cause and fix of the S 400: above (`uniqueItems` in the response_format schema; stripped in the adapter).
`combinations.json` was not changed.

### Spec: llm-probe — V4: granite truncation basis (2026-11-07)

Smoke_Calls used today: 10 of 10 (allowance exhausted for the UTC day). Three granite-4.0-h-micro calls with `--diagnose`.
- Normal D call (declared shape `text`): HTTP 200, `shape_check` mismatch (`result.response missing`), outcome `empty_content`. The response keys were the chat shape: choice keys include `finish_reason` (value `stop`); message keys `content`, `reasoning`, `reasoning_content`, ...; usage 775 in / 7 out, API `neurons` 1.27 (estimate 6). So the premise of V4 is false: granite has `finish_reason`, and the documented `text` shape (`result.response`) is not what the live API returns.
- Fix: `combinations.json` `response_shape` for granite changed `text` -> `chat`. Unit test added (chat envelope, `finish_reason` `length`, completion_tokens = max_tokens -> `truncated`); Property 39 real-combinations test now asserts granite is `chat`. Gates exit 0.
- Truncation probe (`--max-tokens 5`, chat shape): HTTP 200, `finish_reason` `length`, `completion_tokens` 5 = `max_tokens`, outcome `truncated` (fired by the `finish_reason` check; the token-count rule was not the one used). neurons 1.25.
- S call (json_schema `response_format`, uniqueItems stripped): HTTP 200, `finish_reason` `stop`, 1053 in / 18 out, verdict `synthesis` (Output_Validator accepted), 3169 ms, neurons 1.81 (estimate 17). So `json_schema` works for granite with the S schema. Not shown: answer quality (one call).
- Not verified: whether the old `text` shape is ever returned by this model (other REST paths, other parameters); the `text` rule stays in the adapter as a defensive contract with its tests.
- Design updated (Workers AI adapter section, Research findings line, model table, V4 row) with dated notes. Budget counter: requests 10, smoke 10, neurons 181 (includes 65 each reserved for the two failed gemma S calls).

### Spec: llm-probe — V5: DA-8 recomputed (2026-11-07)

- Offline arithmetic only, no API request. Inputs: DA-8 prices and assumptions, `eval/probe/combinations.json` prices, the nine usable Smoke_Calls in `eval/results/smoke/2026-11-07.jsonl`, `budget.ts`. Dev request counts come from the runner's own `buildPlan` over `dev.jsonl` (throwaway scripts in /tmp, not committed): decision 74, synthesis 30, total 104. Locked counts are the documented 244 D + 164 S = 408; the locked cards were not opened.
- The old upper-bound and typical rows (gemma, glm, granite) reproduce from the formula; they were arithmetically right but far above real cost. No reasoning tokens were billed (`reasoning_tokens` null, output tokens 5–160), so the gemma upper bound is not broken.
- Observed per request (API-reported `neurons`, equal to the token formula to six digits): D gemma 7.73, glm 4.48, granite 1.27; S gemma 14.75, glm 8.85, granite 1.81. Reserved estimates: D 31 / 21 / 6, S 65 / 60 / 17. Estimate is 4.0–4.7× observed on D and 4.4–9.4× on S.
- Locked_Run (D only / S only / D and S): observed gemma 1,887 / 2,418 / 4,305, glm 1,093 / 1,451 / 2,544, granite 309 / 296 / 605. Estimate (extrapolated from Dev_Run mean per Task_Type) gemma 7,588 / 11,234 / 18,822, glm 4,953 / 10,184 / 15,138, granite 1,488 / 2,903 / 4,391. Dev_Run estimate gemma 4,355, glm 3,364, granite 981; observed 1,015 / 597 / 148.
- `settle()` replaces the estimate by the usage-derived cost (`ceil` of the token formula), not by the response's `neurons` field. The two are the same number before rounding, so using the field would add no accuracy; only the whole-neuron rounding differs (granite D 2 vs 1.27). The two failed gemma S calls (HTTP 400, no usage) kept their 65-neuron reservation each, as designed.
- Caveat: S output is a single sample per model (160 / 85 / 18 tokens). With S output at the typical 350 tokens the settled gemma D-and-S Locked_Run is 5,232 and glm 4,336: still one UTC day.
- Smoke_Calls: the counter shows 10 of 10 used for 2026-11-07, so no further Smoke_Call is possible that UTC day.
- Design: DA-8 gets a "Revised 2026-11-07 (V5)" note with the observed-cost table, the projection table and the fit analysis. No code changed. Proposed budget values for the Author's approval (task 18): keep per-run 410, daily 600, Neuron_Cap 9,000, smoke 10; follow-up optional (fractional/milli-neuron settlement).

| transformers.js/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4/nvidia | 8fe9dbe3 | 637.5 MiB | ok |
| transformers.js/qwen3.5-2b-q4/nvidia | 315216e8 | 1483.0 MiB | ok |
| transformers.js/lfm2.5-1.2b-instruct-q4/nvidia | 10f72e70 | 814.0 MiB | ok |

- Written: `size.expected_download_bytes` for 5 Combinations in `eval/probe/combinations.json` (only that field). 0 Exclusion_Records appended; `eval/results/exclusions.jsonl` was not created. No limits were changed.

### Spec: llm-probe — V6: ONNX Runtime placement logging (2026-11-07)

- First real capture (task 19.3, Qwen3.5-0.8B, transformers.js 4.3.2, Chrome 155, `logSeverityLevel` 0): ORT prints the "Node placements" block and `parsePlacement` accepts the real format (`WebGpuExecutionProvider` / `CPUExecutionProvider`). Fixture: `eval/probe/tests/unit/fixtures/ort-placement-qwen3.5-0.8b.txt`.
- Decoder session: 3,284 nodes = 3,048 WebGPU + 236 CPU (Gather 64, Unsqueeze 23, Cast 22, Scan 18, Equal 18, Neg 18, Slice 18, Concat 15, Mul/Range/Reshape 12 each, Shape 2, Expand 1, Sub 1). The 18 `Scan` nodes are the gated-delta-net recurrent loops of Qwen3.5's linear-attention layers; they run on the CPU with Memcpy to and from the GPU. The vision encoder was not loaded. This is a finding for the Report.
- Problem: at severity 0 the session logger also logs every WebGPU dispatch ("Starting program ..."): about 848k console lines (110 MB) in 14 minutes, so generation crawled; a `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY` appeared at about 2.2 GB of 4,096 MiB used and the judge did not match it (it looked only at the page error "Invalid Buffer ... previous error").
- Author decision (2026-11-07): (1) the probe session only creates a session with severity 0 / verbose, reads "Node placements", then is released; VRAM is measured only in a quiet session created afterwards (severity 2). (2) Both sessions use the same options except the log level; a hash of them is recorded in the fingerprint and the preflight record, and the code asserts the two option sets differ only in log level. (3) The Report splits CPU-placed operators into shape computation (listed apart, cheap) and compute operators such as `Scan` (latency risk). (4) Cancelled-run numbers are void. Also: the judge matches console OOM lines; an unmatched page error gets a `runtime_error` record with the raw text; every preflight produces a record.
- Method chosen: two sessions. I checked the one-session alternative (verbose session, quiet `RunOptions.logSeverityLevel` per run). onnxruntime `webgpu_context.cc` logs "Starting program" through `context.Logger()` (kernel context logger, plausibly the run logger), but transformers.js 4.3.2 calls `session.run(ortFeed)` with no run options (`src/backends/onnx.js`), so it would need a monkey-patch and the effect could not be verified offline. The real run in part 2 is the decisive check of the two-session method (including that `dispose()` frees the VRAM before the quiet session).
- Two probe defects fixed from the same run: transformers.js 4.3.2 sends a revision-less `/resolve/main/tokenizer_config.json` Range probe (now pinned through `env.fetch`, `pinHubUrl`); huggingface.co now redirects on the same host to `/api/resolve-cache/models/<repo>/<commit>/...` (the pinned-fetch check accepts it only for the pinned commit). The preflight record also gained the hybrid-GPU baseline fields (`baseline_mib`, `idle_min_mib`, `idle_max_mib`, `absolute_peak_mib`, `other_processes`).
- A temporary console dump left in `driver.ts` by the cancelled run was removed.

### Spec: llm-probe — Preflight attempts voided (2026-11-07)

- The cancelled 19.3 run left `eval/results/preflight/attempts.jsonl` (2 invalid attempts), `eval/results/preflight/transformers.js__qwen3.5-0.8b-q4__nvidia.json` (peak 2,132 MiB, generation failed) and `eval/results/exclusions.jsonl` (one `preflight_vram` exclusion for transformers.js/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4/nvidia, "out of memory during generation"). None was committed; all three were deleted.
- Reason: severity 0 logging slowed the run; numbers invalid (VRAM, cold start, latency). The earlier bogus `preflight_load` exclusion set aside before that stays out of the results too.
- The OOM at about 2.2 GB is not a result. Part 2 re-runs the 0.8B preflight with the new method. If the OOM persists in the quiet session, it is the real result (exclusion rule); if it disappears, log "false OOM caused by logging" here.

### Spec: llm-probe — VRAM preflights (2026-11-07)

- Task 19.3 part 2, approved by the Author on 2026-11-07. Headless system Chrome 155, NVIDIA RTX 3050 Ti Laptop (4,096 MiB), hybrid mode. Before every run the card was at 13–14 MiB with only `niri` (2 MiB, graphics). 1,024 new tokens, EOS suppressed, prompt 9,383 bytes (L = 9,378). Run one at a time, 30-minute hard limit each (none hit).
- First 0.8B run (11:20Z): the idle series read 640 → 46 MiB (spread 594 MiB, baseline median 223 MiB). The probe session's VRAM WAS freed by `dispose()`, but with a lag of a few seconds, and the idle series sampled the fall. This was a real defect, not other processes. Fix (`fix(probe)`): `settleMemory` waits until 4 readings, 250 ms apart, are within 20 MiB (at most 30 s) before the idle series. The record also gained `diagnostics` (console line counts per session, "Starting program" count in the quiet session, model/runtime bytes fetched, model file names), and process names are cut to two words (no Chrome flags or profile paths). The first two attempt lines in `attempts.jsonl` were redacted the same way by hand. The 0.8B preflight was repeated twice with the fix (baseline 46 and 44 MiB, idle spread 0).
- The two-session method holds: in every quiet session the console had 2–13 lines and 0 "Starting program" lines (probe session: 4,798 lines for Qwen3.5, 756 for LFM2.5). Placement options hash matched (`placement_options_match: true`) in every run.

| Combination | Peak − baseline | Absolute peak | Baseline (idle min..max) | New tokens | CPU compute / shape ops | Fetched (model) vs screen | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| transformers.js/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4/nvidia (3rd run) | 2,128 MiB | 2,172 MiB | 44 (44..44) | 0 (OOM) | Scan 18 / 218 shape | 637.8 MiB vs 637.5 | excluded: OOM |
| transformers.js/qwen3.5-2b-q4/nvidia (2nd run) | 3,090 MiB | 3,136 MiB | 46 (46..46) | 0 (OOM) | Scan 18 / 218 shape | 1,483.8 MiB vs 1,483.0 | excluded: 3,090 > 2,600 |
| transformers.js/lfm2.5-1.2b-instruct-q4/nvidia | 2,675 MiB | 2,719 MiB | 44 (44..44) | 1,024 in 22.97 s (≈ 44.6 tok/s incl. prefill) | ReduceSum 1 / 35 shape | 812.3 MiB vs 814.0 | excluded: 2,675 > 2,600 |

- OOM conclusion: the 0.8B OOM (`vkAllocateMemory failed with VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY`, ORT `webgpu_context.cc:171`) persists in the quiet session at severity 2, with no logging flood, in both clean runs. It is the real result, not a logging artefact: no "false OOM caused by logging" entry. The OOM came before the first new token, at about 2.2 GB absolute of 4,096 MiB, so a single large allocation failed rather than the card filling up. The 2B hit the same OOM after its peak had already passed the limit.
- Both Qwen3.5 runs requested `onnx/vision_encoder_q4.onnx` and `_data` URLs (`AutoModelForCausalLM`), but the bytes fetched match the screen's text-path sum within 0.3–0.8 MiB, so the encoder weights were not downloaded in full. The ONNX session files reported by the loader were decoder + embed_tokens only. The 2B download (1,483.8 MiB) is under 1,536 MiB: no R9.11 entry.
- First 2B attempt (12:02Z) failed in loading with `TypeError: network error` (transient). The CLI wrote a `preflight_load` / `model_load_failed` exclusion for it. I removed that line from `exclusions.jsonl` because it was not a model result; the attempt stays in `attempts.jsonl`. Open item: a network error should not be classified as `model_load_failed` and should not write an Exclusion_Record.
- The 0.8B Exclusion_Record kept the first run's value ("peak 1959 MiB so far", noisy baseline); the clean runs report `[already in exclusions.jsonl]`. The verdict (OOM) is the same.
- Result: all three transformers.js Combinations are excluded by the VRAM_Preflight on this machine. `attempts.jsonl` has 6 attempts (0.8B ×3, 2B ×2, LFM2.5 ×1).

### Spec: llm-probe — exclusion amendments, report findings and prompt-length diagnosis (2026-11-08)

Done at the Author's request, as the conditions of the approval for task 20.1 (no Dev_Run started).

- Code fix `783efc6` (`fix(probe): do not exclude a Combination on a transient network error`): a `model_load_failed` whose message is a network error (`network error`, `Failed to fetch`, `net::ERR_*`, `ECONNRESET`, ...) is now a `no_verdict` attempt in `attempts.jsonl` and writes no Exclusion_Record. Other load failures still write `preflight_load`. Unit tests added (one end to end with a fake session, one property test). Gates green.
- The earlier misclassified `preflight_load` Exclusion_Record for the 2B (12:02Z, transient `TypeError: network error`) had been deleted from `exclusions.jsonl` by hand on 2026-11-07; its attempt remains in `attempts.jsonl`.
- `eval/results/exclusions.jsonl` amended: Qwen3.5-2B value `3090` -> `out_of_memory` (prefill OOM, new_tokens 0; 3,090 MiB is the reading at the crash, not a full peak). Qwen3.5-0.8B repointed from the voided 11:20:03Z attempt to the formal 11:59:00.635Z preflight (peak 2,128 / absolute 2,172 / baseline 44 MiB, new_tokens 0), value `out_of_memory`. LFM2.5-1.2B unchanged (2,675 > 2,600; limit not relaxed). `probe phase-status` still reads the file.
- Report notes live in `eval/probe/src/report.ts` (`PREFLIGHT_FINDINGS`, section 3, subsection "VRAM_Preflight findings and their limits"); a test in `report.examples.test.ts` checks the text is emitted. The separate NVIDIA and Intel memory columns already existed and were already tested. The LinearAttention statement is attributed to the Author; I did not verify it.
- V2 (task 15.2, transformers.js multimodal text-path Dev_Run) is not applicable: all transformers.js Combinations are excluded. Task 20.3 is therefore not applicable either.
- Prompt-length diagnosis (`eval/probe/scripts/prompt-diagnosis.ts`, raw records in `eval/results/preflight/diagnosis/`; Report only, no Exclusion_Record touched). Qwen3.5-0.8B, transformers.js, NVIDIA, 1,024 new tokens: a 1,560-byte prompt (cut from a Dev_Card prompt) completed with peak 1,392 MiB (absolute 1,438); the longest Dev_Card prompt (4,689 bytes) completed with peak 2,726 MiB (absolute 2,772). Neither ran out of memory; the 9.4 KB preflight prompt did. So the prefill OOM depends on prompt length. The 1 KB variant came out at 1,560 bytes (the system text alone is long), not 1,000.

### Spec: llm-probe — first WebLLM Dev_Run (task 20.2, 2026-11-08)

- Approved by the Author (task 20.1). `probe dev-run --combo webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/nvidia --task DS`, Fresh_Profile, headless Chrome 155 on the RTX 3050 Ti Laptop (hardware adapter returned), 104 requests (74 D + 30 S) on Dev_Cards, no Locked_Cards, no Workers AI. Wall time about 8.4 minutes per run (cold start with empty cache 67.6 s, warm 4.5 s).
- Three runs were made. Runs 1 and 2 (`dev-20261108T012419Z-…`, `dev-20261108T013732Z-…`) graded all 104 outputs Illegal `parse_failure`: every reply began with `<think>\n\n</think>\n\n`, which WebLLM adds for `enable_thinking: false`. Run 2 was a mistake: I fixed the adapter (`f8c5107`, `fix(probe): strip the empty think block WebLLM prepends to replies`, unit test added, gates green) but did not rebuild `eval/probe/dist` (gitignored), so run 2 used the stale page bundle and gave the same result. After `pnpm run page:build`, run 3 (`dev-20261108T014704Z-…`) is the valid one. Runs 1 and 2 stay in `eval/results/dev/` as pre-fix records; run 3 supersedes them. Lesson: `dev-run` does not rebuild the page bundle; run `page:build` after any change under `eval/probe/src`.
- Think-text check (V5/condition 5): run 3, 104 of 104 outputs (97 text, 7 late timeout texts) contain no `think` text at all (0). In runs 1 and 2 all 104 had only the empty block and no thinking content. `enable_thinking: false` works for Qwen3.5-0.8B on WebLLM.
- Dev_Run Metrics (run 3, Dev_Run, not Locked_Run): D acceptable 39/74 (52.7%), 13 Illegal `out_of_set_option` (9 of the D replies carried a leading "- " in the option ID, copied from the bullet list in the prompt); S acceptable 2/30, 7 Illegal `timeout` (10 s), 7 Illegal `malformed_event_id`, 16 valid but wrong. Latency min / median / max 1,512 / 1,948 / 10,003 ms. Contrast pairs consistent 0/10. These are Dev_Run figures for template tuning (task 20.5); they say nothing about the model's Locked_Run score.
- V1 (15.1): no schema-change error across D then S families on one engine. V7 (15.7): fetched 450,403,327 B (429.5 MiB) vs screen 447,173,765 B (+0.7%). Both noted in design.md under the research bullet.
- Memory: NVIDIA device memory.used delta peak 1,769 MiB (absolute 1,813 MiB, baseline 44 MiB, idle 44..44), identical in all three runs. It is one peak per run; the perf record has no per-request series, so I cannot say per prompt length whether VRAM grows. No OOM and no engine fault. Peak is below the 2,600 MiB limit used in the preflight; WebLLM reserves its KV cache for the 4,096-token context at load, which would explain a flat curve, but I did not measure that.

### Spec: llm-probe — WebLLM Qwen3.5-2B Dev_Run and per-request VRAM (task 20.4, 2026-11-08)

- Commit `46a49c3` (`feat(probe): record NVIDIA memory peak per request`): the NVIDIA sampler now keeps a window per Card request (`beginRequest` / `endRequest`, called around `runPrompt` in the driver) and writes `memory.per_request` = `[{prompt_bytes, peak_mib}]` (peak minus the idle baseline, window = samples from request start to one reading after its end) into the perf record. Extra field only; graded output and the Results schema are unchanged. One unit test; gates green. Not available for the earlier 0.8B Dev_Run (it ran before this change; not re-run).
- Task 20.4 scope: all three transformers.js Combinations are excluded (VRAM_Preflight), so the only remaining in-browser Combination is `webllm/qwen3.5-2b-q4f32/nvidia`. Rebuilt the page bundle first (`page:build`). `probe dev-run --combo webllm/qwen3.5-2b-q4f32/nvidia --task DS`, Fresh_Profile, headless Chrome 155, hardware NVIDIA adapter returned, 104 requests (74 D + 30 S) on Dev_Cards, no Locked_Cards, no Workers AI. Run id `dev-20261108T020924Z-webllm-qwen3-5-2b-q4f32-nvidia`. No load failure, no OOM, 0 `engine_fault`, 0 `runtime_error`.
- Time: wall time about 16.5 minutes from start to results (02:09:24Z to about 02:26Z); requests 02:12:00Z to 02:25:43Z. Cold start with empty cache 140.9 s (download window 134.2 s, 63.9 Mbit/s), warm cache 5.9 s.
- Think text: 0 of 104 outputs contain `think` (the adapter strips the empty block, `f8c5107`). V1: D then S families on one engine, no schema-change error.
- Dev_Run Metrics (Dev_Run, not Locked_Run; template hashes `4eea3c06a3478ac09335762ae48b34c4bffd472d58b3dc4944811891dfb838b2` and `c9ebc264d7987785d600010648a1f414297986ffec47f7276b1071df59db8204`): D acceptable 33/74 (0.8B: 39/74), 9 Illegal `out_of_set_option`, 14 severe; S acceptable 3/30 (0.8B: 2/30), 22 Illegal `timeout` (10 s; late answers arrived after 10.3–33.7 s and none of the 22 was acceptable), 0 `malformed_event_id`; contrast pairs consistent 0/10. Latency min / median / max 3,056 / 4,513 / 10,004 ms (D median 3,938 ms; S answers that made it in time 7.2–9.5 s). 0.8B median was 1,948 ms, so the 2B is 2.3x slower and does not beat the 0.8B on D.
- Memory (NVIDIA device memory.used delta): peak 2,461 MiB (absolute 2,505, baseline 44 MiB, idle 44..44), vs 1,769 MiB for the 0.8B. Under the 4,096 MiB card and the 2,600 MiB preflight limit, 139 MiB below the limit.
- VRAM vs prompt length (`per_request`, 104 windows, prompts 2,332–4,689 bytes): window peaks run 2,373–2,461 MiB, a spread of 88 MiB. Typical value 2,383 at 2.3–3.0 KB; 2,415–2,461 at 3.8–4.2 KB; 2,433 at the longest prompt (4,689 B). Only 9 of 104 requests raised the running maximum. So no growth worth the name with prompt length; the footprint is almost all fixed at load (weights plus the KV cache for the 4,096-token context that WebLLM reserves), with a small allocator wobble on top. Caveat: nvidia-smi is sampled every 250 ms; the longest probed prompt is 4.7 KB, far below the 9.4 KB preflight prompt that OOM'd transformers.js.
- V7 (15.7) for the 2B: fetched 1,072,792,109 B model + 13,399,027 B runtime lib = 1,086,191,136 B; model files alone are 1,023.1 MiB. Screen `expected_download_bytes` 1,082,561,095 B (1,032.4 MiB); model bytes are 0.9% under the screen. Far below the 1,536 MiB limit (about 66% of it). Together with the 0.8B (429.5 MiB), V7 is complete for both WebLLM Combinations.

### Spec: llm-probe — V7 conclusion, WebLLM download sizes (task 15.7, 2026-11-08)

- Measured at the pinned revisions on the Target_Machine, empty-cache cold start of a Dev_Run: Qwen3.5-0.8B q4f32_1 fetched 450,403,327 B (429.5 MiB, model + runtime lib), screen 447,173,765 B (+0.7%); Qwen3.5-2B q4f32_1 fetched 1,086,191,136 B (1,035.9 MiB; 1,023.1 MiB of it model files), screen 1,082,561,095 B (+0.3% incl. runtime lib, model files alone -0.9%). Both are under the 1,536 MiB limit (2B at about 67%), so the R9.11 exclusion rule does not fire and no Exclusion_Record is written. The screen is a good predictor (within 1%) for WebLLM.

### Spec: llm-probe — template tuning iteration 1 (task 20.5, 2026-11-08)

- Dev_Cards only, no Locked_Cards, no Workers AI; templates edited in place (`decision.v1.txt`, `synthesis.v1.txt`; file names are fixed in `templates.ts`, the hash changes). No code, schema, validator, grader, timeout or max_tokens change. Unit tests (499) pass; the five Author statements are untouched.
- Change: decision template gets a "How to choose" paragraph (weigh traits, relations, debts/promises/events) and a "How to reply" paragraph (the option ID is the word between "- " and the colon; no dash, no list position, no label). Synthesis template gets "How to write the conclusions" (be brief, short reasons, event ID is only the first word of the event line, person fields are the `#` numbers, first-named person is the holder/owner/actor).
- Template_Hashes: D `6fcfbf99f41c83b0902d25537ed57841c25e1d38e833574930c5164c820e9312`, S `73c8b520f8e91a09db993d769f21094c2ef58826470a2c77a0dbac12755def3c`.
- Dev_Run Metrics (Dev_Run, not Locked_Run), run `dev-20261108T022959Z-webllm-qwen3-5-0-8b-q4f32-nvidia`, 0.8B: D acceptable 43/74 (baseline 39), Illegal 2 (baseline 13; the leading-dash errors are gone), severe 10 (baseline 6); S acceptable 5/30 (baseline 2), Illegal 6 timeout + 6 malformed_event_id (baseline 7 + 7); contrast consistent 0/10; latency median 2,288 ms (baseline 1,948), max 10,002.
- Remaining S failures: on broken_promise / relationship_shift the model still copies the whole event line or a tag text into `evidence`, and puts ticks into person fields; 6 timeouts on debt_chain and broken_promise.

### Spec: llm-probe — template tuning iterations 2–3 and 2B confirmation (task 20.5, 2026-11-08)

- All Dev_Runs below are Dev_Run figures on Dev_Cards (no Locked_Cards, no Workers AI), not Locked_Run scores. Decision template unchanged since iteration 1 (`6fcfbf99…9e9312` = `6fcfbf99f41c83b0902d25537ed57841c25e1d38e833574930c5164c820e9312`).
- Iteration 2 (synthesis only; S hash `5139bcc5a4e10a52184483779dad0f2c4541ac432862170d1749a2c939663d85`): reasons capped at six words, event ID defined as a short code such as e12 with "never copy the rest of the line, its tags or its day", "a day number is never a person". 0.8B, S only (run `dev-20261108T024005Z-…0-8b…`): acceptable 4/30 (iteration 1: 5), Illegal 2 timeout + 4 malformed_event_id (iteration 1: 6 + 6), severe 6, contrast 0/4 pairs, S median 5,553 ms. Timeouts fell; the rest of the S failures are semantic (wrong holder, `pending` vs `broken`, tags used as evidence).
- Iteration 3 (S hash `49b0d0eb27d9b2571a1630704ce13149a101c6e18d4ae851da1d6d5d97d967bc`): added a direction rule (grudge held by the wronged person; promise pending/kept/broken by due day) and "a tag or a day is never an evidence entry". 0.8B, S only (run `dev-20261108T024508Z-…0-8b…`): acceptable 4/30, 5 timeout + 4 malformed_event_id, severe 11 (iteration 2: 6), median 6,391 ms. Worse; reverted to the iteration-2 text. Longer system text raises S latency and did not stop the confusion.
- 2B with iteration-2 templates: DS run `dev-20261108T025126Z-…2b…`: D 25/74 with 20 timeouts, S 2/30 with 23 timeouts. The D timeouts came in groups of four identical 10,001 ms records (the four permutations of one Card run together); a D-only re-run `dev-20261108T0258…-…2b…` (same hashes) gave D 38/74 (baseline 33), Illegal 4 timeout only (no out_of_set_option, baseline 9), severe 12 (baseline 14), median 4,520 ms, so the first run's D timeouts were run-to-run latency noise on a shared laptop GPU (single 9–10 s spikes), not the template. The DS-run S figure (23 timeouts, baseline 22) stands: the 2B needs about 7–10 s for an S reply and the 10 s limit decides.

### Spec: llm-probe — template tuning iteration 4 and stop (task 20.5, 2026-11-08)

- Correction to the previous entry: the 2B D-only re-run is `dev-20261108T031044Z-webllm-qwen3-5-2b-q4f32-nvidia`.
- Iteration 4 (decision only; D hash `84886ec4ee37c9b9a3895202bb72d9508d6fef075b9b06da55e3192ef5b5fc76`): added one sentence preferring the proportionate, least drastic option that keeps obligations unless the facts show strong cause. 0.8B, D only, run `dev-20261108T032233Z-…0-8b…`: acceptable 44/74 (iteration 1: 43), Illegal 2, severe 11 (iteration 1: 10), contrast 0/6 pairs, median 2,321 ms. No gain beyond noise, so the sentence was reverted; the decision template stays at the iteration-1 text. A first attempt at this run (`dev-20261108T032118Z-…`) failed to load the model (`TypeError: Failed to fetch`, a transient network error, one record in `eval/results/failures.jsonl`, no Card answered) and was repeated.

### Spec: llm-probe — template freeze approved (task 20.6, 2026-11-08)

- The Author approved the freeze of the iteration-2 templates. Verified before logging: `sha256sum eval/probe/templates/*` gives D `6fcfbf99f41c83b0902d25537ed57841c25e1d38e833574930c5164c820e9312` and S `5139bcc5a4e10a52184483779dad0f2c4541ac432862170d1749a2c939663d85`, equal to the approved values (iteration 4 was reverted in `e8155c7`). Spec-fixed values (10 s timeout, max_tokens, output schema) are unchanged.
- There is no separate frozen-hash setting in the code. The R3.12 precondition (`devRunPrecondition` in `run.ts`) compares the current Template_Hashes with the most recent Dev_Run of each Combination, so the freeze takes effect through the final Dev_Runs with these hashes (below) and through this entry. Any later change to a template file makes a Locked_Run refuse to start (`template_mismatch`).
- Intel iGPU Combination `webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/intel` added to `eval/probe/combinations.json`: copy of the NVIDIA 0.8B entry (same pins, sampling, constraint, size), `power_preference: low-power`, `phase1_locked: false`. `probe screen` (dry run) lists it as ok (426.5 MiB); `phase-status` ignores it (not locked). The Report already keeps NVIDIA and Intel memory in separate columns (`memoryCells`), and a unit test covers it.
- Author's note 3: Phase_2 must assume both D and S may go to Workers AI. Recorded as a dated note in `design.md` (Phase_2 branch B bullet). Budget figures for D and S together (default gemma-4-26b-a4b-it: 7,576 typical / 15,282 upper bound neurons from DA-8, per-run cap 410, daily cap 600, Neuron_Cap 9,000) are presented at approval time in tasks 22 and 23. I did not add a `probe` command that estimates from the cards: it would need the Locked_Cards' prompts, which are not opened before a Locked_Run. No Workers AI request was sent.

### Spec: llm-probe — final Dev_Runs with the frozen templates (task 20.6, 2026-11-08)

- All figures below are Dev_Run figures on Dev_Cards (no Locked_Cards, no Workers AI), not Locked_Run scores. Each run used D `6fcfbf99…e9312` and S `5139bcc5…63d85` (checked in each Results header), a Fresh_Profile, headless Chrome 155, the three Headless_Launch_Flags, 104 requests (74 D + 30 S), 0 engine faults, no load failure (no retry needed). I ran `page:build` first. Before the runs `nvidia-smi` listed no compute processes on the RTX 3050 Ti (only the compositor `niri`, 2 MiB graphics; 14 MiB used, 5% utilisation); about 34 browser processes were running on the machine (the Author's session), none using the GPU as a compute app.
- Random baseline for D, checked by me from `dev.jsonl` (acceptable options / options per card): over the 18 Scored D cards 10/18 = 55.6%; over all 20 D cards (2 Sanity) 55.0%; per request, weighting each card by its requests (4 per Scored_Card, 1 per Sanity_Card): 41/74 = 55.4% (72 Scored requests: 40/72 = 55.6%). These equal the Author's 55.6% and about 55.4%; no correction needed. The Report now computes them from the cards (`diagnosis.ts`), not from typed constants.
- The Author's reading, recorded as a finding in the Report: the iteration-2 Dev_Runs 0.8B 43/74 and 2B 38/74 are at random level (expected 41 of 74 under a uniform random pick; 58.1% and 51.4% against 55.4%), contrast-pair consistency is 0 of the pairs, and the gain over the first template came mainly from fewer Illegal outputs (0.8B: 13 → 2; 2B: 9 → 4), not from better judgement.
- Qwen3.5-0.8B, q4f32_1, NVIDIA (`dev-20261108T035336Z-…-0-8b-q4f32-nvidia`): D request level 43/74 acceptable (58.1%; random baseline 55.4%), base-Permutation 9/18 (50.0%; baseline 55.6%), severe 5 (base cards), Illegal 2, flips 12/54, contrast pairs 0/6, latency p50/p95 2,245/2,623 ms; S 4/30 requests acceptable (base 1/12), 6 Illegal, contrast 0/4, p50/p95 5,527/8,408 ms. Cold start 92.4 s, warm 4.7 s. NVIDIA memory peak 1,761 MiB.
- Qwen3.5-2B, q4f32_1, NVIDIA (`dev-20261108T040130Z-…-2b-q4f32-nvidia`): D 34/74 (45.9%; baseline 55.4%), base 8/18 (44.4%), severe 2, Illegal 8, flips 11/54, contrast 0/6, p50/p95 4,488/9,238 ms; S 2/30 (base 1/12), 23 Illegal (Timeouts), contrast 0/4, p50/p95 8,692/9,723 ms. Cold start 241.9 s (download 1,072,780,952 B model bytes), warm 6.3 s. NVIDIA memory peak 2,461 MiB. The earlier D-only run with the same hashes gave 38/74; 34/74 here (8 Illegal vs 4) is run-to-run latency noise, not a template change.
- Qwen3.5-0.8B, q4f32_1, Intel iGPU (`webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/intel`, `dev-20261108T041937Z-…-0-8b-q4f32-intel`): the adapter was the Intel integrated GPU (vendor `intel`, architecture `gen-12lp`, not a fallback adapter, `power_preference: low-power`). Outputs graded exactly like the NVIDIA run: D 43/74, base 9/18, Illegal 2, flips 12/54, contrast 0/6; S 4/30, 6 Illegal. Latency D p50/p95 2,243/2,613 ms (NVIDIA 2,245/2,623; same speed within 1%), S 5,536/8,377 ms. Cold start 116.3 s, warm 4.6 s. Memory: 783 MiB, quantity "Chrome GPU process peak RSS (excludes unmapped GEM buffers)"; this is not comparable with the NVIDIA `memory.used` figures and sits in the separate Intel column of the Report. The Intel run being as fast as the NVIDIA one is surprising; I did not look into it (possible reasons: the 0.8B decode is bound by something other than the GPU, or the NVIDIA GPU is slowed by the shared laptop power state; not measured).
- Position and shuffle diagnosis (Author's note 2; read-only on existing outputs, no template change; code in `eval/probe/src/diagnosis.ts`, printed in the Report under the Dev_Run section). Shown positions are the order in the request's prompt; "original" is the card order. Frozen-hash runs, 4-option cards (about 70 valid picks of 72):
  - 0.8B (NVIDIA and Intel identical): by original position 23 / 7 / 30 / 10; by shown position 12 / 22 / 15 / 21 (uniform would be 17.5 each). 12 of 18 cards kept the same option in all four orders; 0 of 18 kept the same shown position. Flip rate against the base pick (both picks valid): 10 of 52.
  - 2B: by original position 35 / 1 / 18 / 10 (64 picks); by shown position 12 / 15 / 15 / 22. 11 of 16 cards kept the same option in every order; 0 of 16 the same shown position. Flip rate 7 of 46.
  - Earlier 0.8B runs: iteration 1 (43/74) 23 / 7 / 30 / 10; iteration 4 (44/74) 24 / 6 / 26 / 14; baseline templates 16 / 10 / 27 / 8. Earlier 2B runs: 28 / 3 / 19 / 14, 28 / 0 / 16 / 8, 36 / 3 / 18 / 11.
  - Reading: the models do not follow the shown position (no card keeps one shown position across orders), and most cards keep the same option across orders (11–12 of 16–18), so the picks are driven by option content, not by where it is listed. But they are driven consistently toward the wrong content: they rarely pick the card's second option (original position 2: 7/70 and 1/64) and often the first and third. In `dev.jsonl` the 4-option cards put the severe option at original position 4 in 15 of 18 cards, acceptable options mostly at positions 1 and 2 (14, 15, 9, 2 for positions 1–4); the models do avoid the last option (10/70, 10/64), but not in favour of the acceptable ones. So this looks like "unable to judge" plus a stable content preference, not a position preference. Caveat: the flip rate is measured over only four of the 24 possible orders, and original position is confounded with option content in these cards (the key is not randomised), which I cannot separate with this data. Whether the locked set has the same layout I did not check, and will not.
- Report: `probe report` now prints, for every D Dev_Run, the request-level acceptable count against the computed random baseline, the shuffle counts and the position tables, and the Dev_Run table shows the card-level random baseline next to each D score. `probe report` loads both card splits as it always did (it parses `locked.jsonl` in code to build the Report; I did not print or read its contents); the generated `docs/llm-probe.md` was not committed.
- `probe phase-status` still lists `webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/nvidia` as finished through a failure record (the transient network error of the 20.5 iteration-4 attempt in `failures.jsonl`) while the same Combination just completed three Dev_Runs. That would stop Phase_1 from running its Locked_Run if the gate is read literally; not changed here. Needs the Author's decision before task 21.

### Spec: llm-probe — the Intel Dev_Run ran on the NVIDIA GPU; guard and fix (task 20.6, 2026-11-08)

- Trigger: the Author noticed that the Intel iGPU Dev_Run (`dev-20261108T041937Z-webllm-qwen3-5-0-8b-q4f32-intel`) graded exactly like the NVIDIA 0.8B run and had the same latency. My previous entry had noted this and called it surprising without looking into it; that was the wrong call.
- Read-only evidence from existing data: all 104 outcomes are identical to the NVIDIA run (`dev-20261108T035336Z-…-nvidia`), per-request latency differs by a median of 11 ms (max 1,877 ms). The Intel perf record has only the Chrome GPU process RSS (783 MiB), no NVIDIA reading, so "did NVIDIA memory rise" could not be answered from that record. A standalone WebGPU check with the harness's Chrome flags (a 256 MiB buffer) did honour `powerPreference`: `low-power` gave vendor `intel` and NVIDIA memory.used stayed at 44 MiB; `high-performance` gave `nvidia` and 313 MiB. The Chrome GPU process shows up as a 4 MiB NVIDIA compute app in both cases, so a process entry alone is not evidence. The launch has no PRIME / Vulkan ICD variables; the flags are the three Headless_Launch_Flags. So the browser mapping was fine; the engine was the problem.
- Direct measurement: I re-ran the Intel Combination on the unfixed code with an external `nvidia-smi` log every 2 s. NVIDIA memory.used went from 57 to 1,743 MiB at 100% utilisation during the run. Conclusion: the work ran on the NVIDIA GPU although the page reported the Intel adapter.
- Root cause: `navigator.gpu.requestAdapter` is called twice. The page's adapter check passes the Combination's `low-power`; WebLLM 0.2.87 then calls `detectGPUDevice()` whose `powerPreference` defaults to `"high-performance"` and creates its own adapter and device (`web-llm/lib/index.js`, `detectGPUDevice`). The adapter strings in the Results header came from the page's call, not from the device that ran the model. WebLLM has no option to pass the preference.
- Code, commit `b547724` (`fix(probe)`): (1) `pinLowPowerAdapter` in `adapters/gpu.ts`, called by the page's adapter check, wraps `navigator.gpu.requestAdapter` so every call carries `low-power` for a `low-power` Combination; `high-performance` Combinations are untouched, so earlier NVIDIA data stays comparable. (2) Guard: an Intel run now also samples NVIDIA memory.used (`IntelWithNvidiaWatch`); the perf record gets `memory.device_mismatch` (true when the NVIDIA rise exceeds 256 MiB, null when nvidia-smi gave no reading) and `memory.nvidia_watch`. (3) The Report shows a mismatch run as `INVALID: … ran on the NVIDIA GPU` in the GPU and Intel-memory columns, never as an Intel figure. `eval/results/invalid-dev-runs.json` lists runs found invalid afterwards (the committed result files stay as written). Tests: 4 for the guard (flat, rise, no reading, threshold edge), 1 for the pinning, 1 for the Report cell, 1 for the invalid list. Unit tests 514 pass (Property 32 failed once under load and passed on two re-runs and alone; I did not find a cause), Type_Check passes, `scripts/gates.sh` green. I did not inject a fault to see the guard fail separately: the unfixed run itself is that test (below).
- Guard on the unfixed code: `dev-20261108T043623Z-…-intel` (page bundle without the fix): `device_mismatch: true`, NVIDIA delta 1,761 MiB (the NVIDIA run's own peak is 1,761 MiB), D 43/74 requests acceptable, 9/18 base, 2 Illegal (as before), D p50/p95 2,231/2,608 ms. Both unfixed runs are marked invalid in `invalid-dev-runs.json`.
- Corrected Intel Dev_Run, `dev-20261108T044612Z-webllm-qwen3-5-0-8b-q4f32-intel` (page rebuilt with the fix, Fresh_Profile, `--task DS`, 104 requests, 0 engine faults): adapter `intel` / `gen-12lp`, `device_mismatch: false`, NVIDIA delta 4 MiB (absolute 48, baseline 44; external log 42–57 MiB at 0%). Intel GPU process peak RSS 492 MiB. Cold start 117.0 s, warm 8.4 s. Now it is slow: all 74 D requests hit the 10 s timeout; the late answers arrived after 11,975 / 14,955 / 17,495 ms (min / median / max), about 6.7x the NVIDIA median of 2,245 ms. S: 3 timeouts and 27 `runtime_error` ("The current Object has already been disposed", 0–1 ms) after the repeated timeouts; I did not look into why the engine ends up disposed after timeouts on the slow device (not an `engine_fault` by the harness's count). So no Intel quality figure exists for the frozen templates: every on-time answer is missing. What the run shows is that the Intel iGPU cannot meet the 10 s limit at all for the 0.8B (a latency finding, not a quality one).
- Earlier figures that came from the unfixed Intel runs (43/74, 9/18, p50 2,243 ms, S 4/30) are NVIDIA figures duplicated, not Intel figures; the 20.6 entry above that quotes them as Intel is superseded by this one. The NVIDIA 0.8B and 2B runs are unaffected (high-performance, not wrapped).
- Not changed: `phase-status` / `failures.jsonl` question from the previous entry still waits for the Author.

### Spec: llm-probe — phase-gate fix, Locked_Run transient rules, Author's approvals for 21.2 and 21.3 (task 21.1, 2026-11-08)

- Gate fix, option A (Author), commit `d8066ee` (`fix(probe)`). Before: `hasFailure` counted any record in `failures.jsonl`, so the transient `TypeError: Failed to fetch` of the Dev_Run at 2026-11-08T03:22:28.525Z finished Phase_1 for `webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/nvidia` while the same Combination had since completed three Dev_Runs. Now `phase.ts` counts a failure record only when its run type is `locked` AND `transient` is false. The schema stays `llm-probe-failure-v1`; `run_kind` (it was already written by the run flow) and the new `transient` flag are optional fields, so every older line still parses.
- Legacy reading rule (`effectiveFailure` in `results.ts`): `run_kind` is the field if it is `dev` or `locked`; else a `run_id` that starts with `dev-` means dev; anything else, including no run ID, means locked (fail-safe: a failure that cannot be shown to be a Dev_Run is not ignored). `transient` is the field if boolean; else the transient-error matcher is applied to `detail`, `reason` and `message`. The matcher is the one from `783efc6`, moved unchanged to `eval/probe/src/transient.ts` (`preflight.ts` re-exports it). New records always carry `transient` (`appendFailure` fills it from the matcher when the caller gave none).
- Correction record: the original line of 03:22:28.525Z in `eval/results/failures.jsonl` is untouched. A second line was appended with schema `llm-probe-failure-correction-v1` (`combination`, `target_at`, `run_kind: dev`, `transient: true`, `reason`, `at`). `readFailures` merges corrections by `combination` + `target_at` (last one wins) and does not return the correction lines as failures. A test reads the committed file and asserts the original bytes are there and the record reads as dev + transient. Note: by the legacy rule alone this record already read as dev (its `run_id` starts with `dev-`) and transient (matcher); the correction makes it explicit.
- `probe phase-status` after the fix: Phase_1 in progress; `transformers.js/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4`, `transformers.js/qwen3.5-2b-q4`, `transformers.js/lfm2.5-1.2b-instruct-q4` finished by Exclusion_Record; `webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/nvidia` and `webllm/qwen3.5-2b-q4f32/nvidia` pending (needs D and S). The Report's Combination status and "Failed Combinations" list count only gate-relevant failures; dev/transient records are still listed, marked "not counted as a Combination failure".
- Locked_Run transient-error rules (Author's condition 2). Decided and implemented before any Locked_Run (`LOCKED_LOAD_RETRIES`, `LOCKED_LOAD_BACKOFF_MS` in `run.ts`):
  1. Automatic retry applies only to the model load of a Locked_Run, before the first Card request, and only when the load fails with `model_load_failed` and a message the transient matcher recognises (`network error`, `Failed to fetch`, `net::ERR_*`, `ECONNRESET`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `socket hang up`).
  2. At most 3 retries (4 loads in all), with a wait of 5 s, 15 s and 45 s before retries 1, 2 and 3.
  3. Every retry closes the failed handle and opens a new one with a new Fresh_Profile (the Fresh_Profile hook runs again). A retry never reuses a half-filled cache or the byte counter of a failed attempt, which is why a plain second `load()` on the same handle was rejected: the counter is not reset, so the download figures would be tainted.
  4. Cold-start and download figures come only from the first load that succeeded, in the Fresh_Profile opened for that load. Failed attempts write no perf record. When more than one load was needed, the perf record carries `load_attempts` (the count), so the Report reader can see it.
  5. Each failed attempt writes a failure record `run_kind: locked`, `transient: true`, `will_retry: true`, `attempt: n`. The gate ignores it. These are attempt notes, not Combination failures.
  6. If the last retry also fails transiently, the run stops as any load failure does (not_run records, ledger abort `model_load_failed`, a final locked + transient failure record the gate ignores). The Combination is not marked failed. Continuing it is `locked-run --resume <entry-id>`; no new Author approval mechanism is added, and per task 21.6 the resume is made only after asking the Author again.
  7. A transient error after the first request (mid-run, for example a network error reported as a load-class error) is never retried automatically: the run stops, the failure record is flagged transient (so the gate ignores it), and the run is continued only with `--resume`, after asking the Author again. A Fresh_Profile failure before the ledger `start` is not retried by this rule and, unchanged, uses no once-only slot.
  8. A load failure that is not transient (for example `weights missing`, out of memory) is not retried and counts for the gate, as before.
  Tests: `tests/unit/run.transient.test.ts` (two transient failures then success: 2 sleeps, 3 handles, 3 Fresh_Profiles, perf from the third handle with `load_attempts: 3`, gate ignores both records; no retry beyond the limit: 4 loads, ledger abort, no perf file; non-transient: no retry, counted; mid-run transient: stops, not counted; first-try load: no `load_attempts`). The browser retry path itself (a real second Chrome) is not exercised by a test; the fake runtime stands in for it.
- Report notes (Author's condition 3), `DEV_CONFOUND_NOTE` in `report.ts`, printed at the start of the Dev_Run section and tested: in the dev set the severe option is last in card order for every card that has one, because option rotation was applied only to the locked set (an oversight on the card-authoring side; the cards are locked and unchanged). Per the Author, the locked set's severe options are last 14, middle 26, first 7; so the position-versus-content distinction is to be drawn from the Locked_Run results. I did not open or compute anything from the locked files. Check of `dev.jsonl` only: 20 D cards, 18 with four options and 2 unscored two-option Sanity_Cards; 15 scored four-option cards have exactly one severe option, and it is the fourth in card order for all 15; the 3 other four-option cards have none. The two Sanity_Cards also list their severe option last (17 D cards in total with a severe option, all last). So the earlier "15 of 18 four-option cards" and the Author's "15 D cards with a severe option, all last" agree; the only difference is that the Sanity_Cards add 2 unscored cards with the same pattern.
- Author's approvals recorded here (task 21.1 dispatch): Locked_Run 21.2 (`webllm/qwen3.5-0.8b-q4f32/nvidia`) and Locked_Run 21.3 (`webllm/qwen3.5-2b-q4f32/nvidia`), each `--task DS`, at most 408 requests. These are the approvals of the start; neither run was started in this entry. The start and end entries of each run come when it runs.
- Author's condition 4: after the Locked_Runs, I report the Phase_1 conclusion first, and only then ask for Phase_2 approval, with a budget estimate that covers both D and S on Workers AI.
- No Locked_Run was started and no Workers AI call was made. `eval/cards/` and `locked.jsonl` were not opened (only `dev.jsonl` for the confound check).

Dev and Locked together are 11,618 at the upper bound, above the 9,000 Neuron_Cap, so the Dev_Run goes today (UTC) and the Locked_Run starts at 00:00 UTC on a later fresh day.

### Spec: llm-probe — Report figure inconsistencies checked on the gemma Dev_Run (2026-11-08)

- Found by the Author in `dev-20261108T101109Z-workers-ai-gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`; none was a calculation bug, all three were unlabelled definition differences:

  - (c) S latency 4,186/8,288 vs hand 4,480/6,886: the Report uses the design's nearest-rank method (index floor((p·n+99)/100)−1) over all 30 S requests with text; recomputing that from the records gives the same numbers. The hand figures used a different index rule.

### Spec: llm-probe — task 28 Raw_Choice validator mode and `llm_render_system_one` (2026-11-09 05:51Z)

- `llm_validate` takes an optional `mode` (`json` default, `raw_choice`). In `raw_choice` mode `raw` is a Raw_Choice: `Decision{option}` exactly when it equals an option ID byte for byte, else `Illegal{OutOfSetOption}` (path null, no trimming, no JSON). An S context or an unknown or non-string `mode` is an `input` error; `outcome` inputs (timeout, error) are handled as before in both modes. `simwasm.ts` `llmValidate(input, mode, cardId?)` takes `mode` as a required argument and inserts it into the JSON text without re-serializing it (`withMode`); the existing callers pass `'json'`.
- New export `llm_render_system_one` (api.rs, one `#[wasm_bindgen]` fn, `llmRenderSystemOne` in simwasm.ts). The mapping parser and assembly are in the new `crates/sim-core/src/llm/system_one.rs`; `render.rs` gained `render_state`, `criteria` and `reject_placeholders` that reuse the existing fact-block functions. `RENDERER_VERSION` stays 2; no existing export result changed.
- Property 42 (4 tests) and Property 41 Rust part (4 tests) in `llm_properties.rs`. Generators produce some contexts that the card schema rejects (an event with no actors), so the tests repair that fault and skip contexts the API still rejects (`api_accepts`).
- New golden `fixtures/grading/system-one-golden.json` (new `sim-cli grading-system-one-golden`, run by `scripts/regen-grading-golden.sh`), covering `raw_choice` validation and `llm_render_system_one` for all 34 Dev_Cards, with a synthetic mapping embedded in the generator. `dev-golden.json`, `dev-outputs.json` and `fixtures/parity/` are byte-identical (`git diff --stat` empty). Parity: `llm_parity.rs` (native and wasm32, names Card ID and export), the TypeScript `parity.test.ts`, and the sim-cli regeneration test. Property 15 bad-input test covers the new export and the `raw_choice` mode.
- `sim_wasm_bg.wasm`: 628,252 bytes now, against 615,000 recorded at the end of the earlier probe work; the Size_Limit is 26,214,400 bytes. Full `scripts/gates.sh` green, including the Smoke_Test.
- Fault injection: `raw_choice` mode made to trim ASCII whitespace before comparing. `scripts/gates.sh` failed at `cargo test --workspace` (exit 101), but the first failing binary was the sim-cli test `grading_generators_are_deterministic_and_match_the_committed_fixtures` (`fixtures/grading/system-one-golden.json differs from the generator output`), because the workspace runs sim-cli before sim-core and stops at the first failing binary. Running `cargo test -p sim-core --test llm_properties raw_choice` by hand then failed with a Property 42 counterexample: shrunk to the Raw_Choice `" defer_to_magistrate"` (leading space) getting `Decision` instead of `Illegal{out_of_set_option}`; the 1 MiB test failed too. Fault reverted; gates green again.
- Deviation noted: the design's mapping format has `[meta]`, `[instructions]`, `[state]`; task 30.1 names `[state]`, `[instructions]`, `[criteria]`. The parser takes `[instructions]` and `[state]` as required and `[meta]` and `[criteria]` as optional (`[criteria]` text ignored), so either file form renders. Task 30 should settle one.
- No run, no Workers AI request; `eval/cards/` and Results files untouched; locked.jsonl, review/locked.md, blind/* and `env` not opened.

### Spec: llm-probe — task 29 System One adapter, truncation checks, budget estimate, Decision_Model smoke (2026-11-09 05:40Z)

- Added `eval/probe/src/adapters/system-one.ts`: request body built from an ordered list (`{model, state, questions: {decision: {type: "choice", instructions, criteria}}}`, `criteria` as an id-to-label object in Permutation order; an integer-like or repeated option ID is refused), `POST /ai/run/@cf/cloudflare/<model>`, the Raw_Choice read from `result.answers.decision.choice` only (a missing member or a non-string `choice` is `system_one_shape`), the byte-exact body kept in a separate `systemOne.responseText` field (`confidence` and `probabilities` are never read), usage from `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` (missing or non-integer: `usage_reported: false`). Pre-check `body_bytes > W` refuses with nothing sent (`over_context`); post-check `truncated_state` on a `truncated: true` flag (envelope, `result` or `usage`), `input_tokens < floor(body_bytes / K)` or `input_tokens > W`. `workers-ai.ts` now exports its timeout and POST glue (`runWithTimeout`, `postJson`) and the HTTP error mapping (`openEnvelope`), with no behaviour change; its tests are untouched.
- `combinations.json`: `workers-ai/clef-flash` (24,576 tokens, 3,455 neurons per million) and `workers-ai/clef` (65,536, 21,818), `response_shape: "system_one"`, `validator_mode: "raw_choice"`, `max_bytes_per_token: 8` (provisional, part of the fingerprint), `phase1_locked: false`. `parseCombinations` refuses a `system_one` record without `validator_mode`, and a `SystemOneCombination` without it fails the Type_Check (a `@ts-expect-error` example). Mapping file path is in the records; the file itself is task 30.
- `budget.ts`: `estimateSystemOneNeurons` (`ceil((bytes + 256) × price / 1e6)`, `BigInt`, no output term) and `BudgetSession.settleSystemOne`. `results.ts`: `over_context` not_run reason, `truncated_state` engine fault code, optional `system_one` record member. `run.ts`: `gateRun` sends Decision_Models through `allowDecisionModel`; `mapResult` writes a `truncated_state` engine fault and the `system_one` field. `smoke.ts`: a Decision_Model Smoke_Call (D Dev_Cards only, no approval) with `checkSystemOneShape`, `shape_check`, `declared_shape`, HTTP status and `bytes_per_token` as `{numerator, denominator}`. `run-command.ts` picks the System_One runtime for such a Combination.

Four existing tests had to change because the real files changed: the Combination count (9 to 11) and Workers AI count in `templates.test.ts`, the same filter in the P39 real-file test, and the `env.ts` import allow-list in `no-env-read.test.ts`.
